\displaystyle\nabla u+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u.

Step 5.
The asymptotic estimate as
|n|\rightarrow\infty
.
Now, recalling that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}=\partial_{r}^{2}+\frac{(2m-1)}{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)=\left(\alpha+(m-1)+n\right)\left(\alpha+(m-1)-n\right)r^{\alpha-2}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right).

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)=\bigg(\left(\alpha+(m-1)+n\right)\left(\alpha+(m-1)-n\right)r^{\alpha-2}\cos(\beta r)+2\,\partial_{r}\left(r^{\alpha}\right)\partial_{r}\left(\cos(\beta r)\right)

\displaystyle\left.+r^{\alpha}\partial_{r}^{2}\left(\cos(\beta r)\right)+r^{\alpha}\frac{2m-1}{r}\partial_{r}\left(\cos(\beta r)\right)\right)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\left(\left(\alpha+(m-1)+n\right)\left(\alpha+(m-1)-n\right)-\beta^{2}\right)r^{\alpha-2}\cos(\beta r)+\beta\left(2\alpha+2m-1\right)r^{\alpha-1}\sin(\beta r)\Big)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right).

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\left(\left(\alpha+(m-1)+n\right)\left(\alpha+(m-1)-n\right)-\beta^{2}\right)r^{\alpha-2}\sin(\beta r)

\displaystyle+\beta\left(2\alpha+2m-1\right)r^{\alpha-1}\cos(\beta r)\Big)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right).

Since

\displaystyle u_{n}=r^{1+\alpha_{m,n}}\cos\left(\beta_{m,n}r\right)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a_{n}e^{in\theta}\right)+r^{1+\alpha_{m,n}}\sin\left(\beta_{m,n}r\right)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(b_{n}e^{in\theta}\right)

\displaystyle+r^{1-\alpha_{m,n}}\cos\left(\beta_{m,n}r\right)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(c_{n}e^{in\theta}\right)+r^{1-\alpha_{m,n}}\sin\left(\beta_{m,n}r\right)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(d_{n}e^{in\theta}\right),

we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{m}u_{n}=\left(\alpha_{m,n}+m+n\right)\left(\alpha_{m,n}+m-n\right)r^{\alpha_{m,n}-1}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a_{n}\,e^{in\theta}\right)+\left(\alpha_{m,n}+m+n\right)\left(\right)

On the other hand, we have
\beta_{m,n}\leq 2m(m-2)
, so we get

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{m}+m+n\right)\left(\alpha_{m,n}+m-n\right)-\beta_{m,n}^{2}=\left(\alpha_{m,n}+m\right)^{2}-\left(n^{2}+\beta_{m,n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{m,n}^{2}+2m\,\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}-\left(n^{2}+\beta_{m,n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{m,n}+(m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1}{2}+2m\,\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}-\left(n^{2}+\frac{\mu_{m,n}-\left((m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1\right)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2m\,\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1>2m\sqrt{n^{2}+(m-1)^{2}}+2(m(m-1)+1)>2m|n|+2(m(m-1)+1).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\left(-\alpha_{m,n}+m+n\right)\left(-\alpha_{m,n}+m-n\right)-\beta_{m,n}^{2}=\alpha_{m,n}^{2}-2m\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}-\left(n^{2}+\beta_{m,n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{m,n}+(m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1}{2}-2m\,\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}-\left(n^{2}+\frac{\mu_{m,n}-\left((m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1\right)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-2m\,\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1=\frac{-4m^{2}\alpha_{m,n}^{2}+\left(m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1\right)^{2}}{4m^{2}\alpha_{m,n}+m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle-4m^{2}\alpha_{m,n}^{2}+\left(m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=-2m^{2}\left(\mu_{m,n}+(m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1\right)+m^{4}+(m-1)^{4}+1+2m^{2}(m-1)^{2}+2m^{2}+2(m-1)^{2}

\displaystyle=-2m^{2}\mu_{m,n}-2m^{2}n^{2}+m^{4}+(m-1)^{4}+2(m-1)^{2}.

Therefore, we have
-4m^{2}\alpha_{m,n}^{2}+\left(m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1\right)^{2}<0
if and only if

\displaystyle 2m^{2}(\mu_{m,n}+n^{2})>m^{4}+(m-1)^{4}+(m-1)^{2}.

Using the inequality
\mu_{m,n}>n^{2}+m(m-2)
, we get

\displaystyle 2m^{2}(\mu_{m,n}+n^{2})>4m^{2}n^{2}+2m^{3}(m-2)\geq m^{4}+(m-1)^{4}+(m-1)^{2}\Longleftrightarrow n^{2}>2-\frac{2}{m}+\frac{3}{4m^{2}}.

Notice that the right-hand side is always lower than
2
(since
m\geq 3/2
), and since
|n|\geq 2
, we actually get

\displaystyle-4m^{2}\alpha_{m,n}^{2}+\left(m^{2}+(m-1)^{2}+1\right)^{2}<-8m^{2}<0.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle 2(1-\alpha_{m,n})+2m-1=-2\alpha_{m,n}+2m+1<0\Longleftrightarrow 2\left(\mu_{m,n}+(m-1)^{2}+n^{2}+1\right)>(2m+1)^{2}

and this holds if and only if

\displaystyle 2\mu_{m,n}+2n^{2}>2m^{2}+8m-3.

Since
\mu_{m,n}>n^{2}+m(m-2)
, this inequality is true in particular if

\displaystyle 4n^{2}>2m^{2}+8m-3-2m(m-2)=12m-3

which only holds for
|n|
large enough. Instead, if
2m^{2}+8m-3-2n^{2}>0
, the inequality becomes

\displaystyle 4\left(n^{4}+(6m(m-2)+4)n^{2}+m^{2}(m-2)^{2}\right)>(2m^{2}+8m-3-2n^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow 32\,m^{2}n^{2}-48\,m^{3}-16\,mn^{2}-36\,m^{2}+4\,n^{2}+48\,m-9>0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow 4(8m^{2}-4m+1)n^{2}-12(4m^{3}+3m^{2}-4m)-9>0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow n^{2}>\frac{12(4m^{3}+3m^{2}-4m)+9}{4(8m^{2}-4m+1)}.

Since
n^{2}<m^{2}+4m-\frac{3}{2}
, this inequality holds for some
n
provided that

\displaystyle\frac{12(4m^{3}+3m^{2}-4m)+9}{4(8m^{2}-4m+1)}<m^{2}+4m-\frac{3}{2}\Longleftrightarrow 32\,m^{4}+64\,m^{3}-144\,m^{2}+88\,m-15>0

which is always true. Now, it is apparent that we must control the following quantity

\displaystyle g(r,\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{a}_{1}\left(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)+\lambda\,r^{\alpha+1}\sin(\beta r)\right)+\mathfrak{a}_{2}\left(r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)+\lambda\,r^{\alpha+1}\cos(\beta r)\right)

\displaystyle=r^{\alpha}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\cos(\beta r)+\mathfrak{a}_{2}\sin(\beta r)\right)+\lambda\,r^{\alpha+1}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\sin(\beta r)+\mathfrak{a}_{2}\cos(\beta r)\right).

where
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. First, we want to refine (
3
). For all
0<\varepsilon<1
and
0<\beta<\infty
, there exists
0<b=b(\varepsilon,\beta)<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &1-\varepsilon\leq\cos(\beta r)\leq 1\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,0<r<b\\
&(1-\varepsilon)\beta r\leq\sin(\beta r)\leq\sin(\beta r)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,0<r<b.\end{aligned}\right.

Then, (
3
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\left|\det\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}(x)&f_{2}(x)\\
f_{1}(y)&f_{2}(y)\end{pmatrix}\right|^{2}dx\,dy=4\pi^{2}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{a}^{b}\sin^{2}\left(\beta(r-s)\right)r^{2\alpha+1}s^{\alpha+1}dr\,ds

\displaystyle\geq 4\pi^{2}(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\beta^{2}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{a}^{b}(r-s)^{2}r^{2\alpha+1}s^{2\alpha+1}dr\,ds.

The next estimate must also be handled more carefully. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{a}^{b}(r-s)^{2}r^{2\alpha+1}s^{2\alpha+1}dr\,ds=\frac{b^{2(2\alpha+3)}}{4(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha+3)}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{b^{2(2\alpha+3)}}{(2\alpha+3)^{2}}\left(1-2\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\alpha+3}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha+3)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)(2\alpha+3)^{2}}b^{2(2\alpha+3)}

\displaystyle\times\left(1-(2\alpha+3)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)+8(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\alpha+3}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha+3)}\right)

Since we computed the integral of a function positive on a set of full measure (since it only vanishes on the diagonal
\Delta=\Omega\times\Omega\cap\left\{(x,y):x=y\right\}
), we deduce in particular that for all
0<a<b<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda\left(\alpha,\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)=1-(2\alpha+3)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)+8(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\alpha+3}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha+3)}>0,

where

\displaystyle\Lambda(\alpha,t)=1-(2\alpha+3)^{2}\left(e^{-2(\alpha+1)t}+e^{-2(\alpha+2)t}\right)+8(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)e^{-(2\alpha+3)t}+e^{-2(2\alpha+3)t},\quad t>0.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\left|\det\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}(x)&f_{2}(x)\\
f_{1}(y)&f_{2}(y)\end{pmatrix}\right|^{2}dx\,dy=4\pi^{2}\beta^{2}(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\times\frac{1}{2(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)(2\alpha+3)}b^{2(2\alpha+3)}\Lambda\left(\alpha,\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi^{2}\beta^{2}(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)(2\alpha+3)}b^{2(2\alpha+3)}\Lambda\left(\alpha,\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right),

while

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|f_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{\pi\beta}{\alpha+1}b^{2\alpha+3}\sqrt{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}\right)\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)},

and we finally get

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{2}\beta^{2}(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)(2\alpha+3)}|\mathfrak{a}_{1}||\mathfrak{a_{2}}|b^{2(2\alpha+3)}\Lambda\left(\alpha,\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi\beta}{\sqrt{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}}b^{2\alpha+3}\sqrt{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}\right)\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}dx,

which becomes

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}_{1}||\mathfrak{a}_{2}|b^{2(2\alpha+3)}

(3.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta\sqrt{(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)}(2\alpha+3)\sqrt{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}\right)\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)}}{\pi(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\left(1-(2\alpha+3)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+1)}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha+2)}\right)+8(\alpha+1)(\alpha+2)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\alpha+3}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha+3)}\right)}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}dx.

Likewise, if
\alpha>2
and

\displaystyle g(r,\theta)=r^{-\alpha}\mathfrak{a}_{1}\cos(\beta r)+r^{-\alpha}\mathfrak{a}_{2}\sin(\beta r),

we have

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{a}_{1}||\mathfrak{a}_{2}|\frac{1}{a^{2(2\alpha-3)}}

(3.28)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta\sqrt{(\alpha-1)(\alpha-2)}(2\alpha-3)\sqrt{\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha-1)}\right)\left(1-\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha-2)}\right)}}{\pi(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\left(1-(2\alpha-3)\left(\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha-1)}+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(\alpha-2)}\right)+8(\alpha-1)(\alpha-2)\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2\alpha-3}\right)+\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)^{2(2\alpha-3)}}\int_{\Omega}|g|^{2}dx.

Now, for
\lambda>0
, we can actually make a direct estimate since the crossed terms yield an almost positive contribution. Indeed, they are equal to

\displaystyle\lambda\,r^{2\alpha+1}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\cos(\beta r)+\mathfrak{a}_{2}\sin(\beta r)\right)\left(\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\sin(\beta r)+\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}\cos(\beta r)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda\,r^{2\alpha+1}\left(\left(|\mathfrak{a}_{1}|^{2}+|\mathfrak{a}_{2}|^{2}\right)\cos(\beta r)\sin(\beta r)+\mathrm{Re}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon)\beta\lambda\,r^{2\alpha+2}+\lambda\,\mathrm{Re}\left(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\overline{\mathfrak{a}_{2}}\right)r^{2\alpha+1}.

(3.29)

However, we will not get the growth in
|n|
in the coefficient so we will instead use
\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{n}
. Recall that

\displaystyle 8\frac{z^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=(\alpha+m+n-1)(\alpha+m+n-3)a\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{in\theta}

\displaystyle+(\alpha+m-n-1)(\alpha+m-n-3)\overline{a}\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{-in\theta}.

Recall also that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}=\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-i\theta}\partial_{r}-i\,e^{-i\theta}\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}\right).

(3.30)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)e^{in\theta})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\alpha+n\right)r^{\alpha-1}\cos(\beta r)-\beta\,r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)\right)e^{i(n-1)\theta}

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\left(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)e^{in\theta}\right)=\frac{1}{4}\left((\alpha+n)(\alpha-1)r^{\alpha-2}\cos(\beta r)+\beta(\alpha+n)r^{\alpha-1}\cos(\beta r)-\alpha\beta r^{\alpha-1}\sin(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\beta^{2}r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)-n\left((\alpha+n)r^{\alpha-1}\cos(\beta r)-\beta\,r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)\right)\right)e^{i(n-2)\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left((\alpha+n)(\alpha+n-2)r^{\alpha-2}\cos(\beta r)-\beta(2\alpha+2n-1)\sin(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)\right)e^{i(n-2)\theta}.

(3.31)

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)e^{in\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left((\alpha+m+n-2)(\alpha+m+n-3)r^{\alpha-2}\cos(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\beta(2\alpha+2m+2n-3)r^{\alpha-1}\sin(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)\right)e^{i(n-2)\theta}.

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)e^{in\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left((\alpha+m+n-2)(\alpha+m+n-3)r^{\alpha-2}\sin(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\beta(2\alpha+2m+2n-3)r^{\alpha-1}\cos(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)\right)e^{i(n-2)\theta},

and finally

\displaystyle 8\frac{z^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\cos(\beta r)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)=\left((\alpha+m+n-1)(\alpha+m+n-3)\cos(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\beta(2\alpha+2m+2n-3)r\sin(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{2}\cos(\beta r)\right)a\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{in\theta}

\displaystyle+\left((\alpha+m-n-1)(\alpha+m-n-3)\cos(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\beta(2\alpha+2m-2n-3)r\sin(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{2}\cos(\beta r)\right)a\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{-in\theta}

and

\displaystyle 8\frac{z^{2}}{|z|^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{m}\left(r^{\alpha}\sin(\beta r)\mathrm{Re}\,\left(a\,e^{in\theta}\right)\right)=\left((\alpha+m+n-1)(\alpha+m+n-3)\sin(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\beta(2\alpha+2m+2n-3)r\cos(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{2}\sin(\beta r)\right)a\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{in\theta}

\displaystyle+\left((\alpha+m-n-1)(\alpha+m-n-3)\sin(\beta r)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\beta(2\alpha+2m-2n-3)r\cos(\beta r)-\beta^{2}r^{2}\sin(\beta r)\right)a\,r^{\alpha-2}e^{-in\theta}.

Therefore, we see that the main coefficient behaves likes
n^{2}
, which will yield a
n^{3}
growth at infinity if it were not for the crossed terms. Taking them into account and using inequalities, as
\alpha_{m,n}=|n|+m(m-2)+1+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)
, we see by inequalities (
3.27
) and (
3.28
) (see also (
3
)) that we will only get an inequality of the type (where
\Gamma
is a universal constant)

\displaystyle|a_{n}||b_{n}|\leq\Gamma\,n^{2}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{n}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx,

which in turns will imply by the same argument in the
Step 4
that

\displaystyle\pi|n|\left(|a_{n}|^{2}b^{2\alpha_{m,n}}+|b_{n}|^{2}b^{2(\alpha_{m,n}+1)}+|c_{n}|^{2}\frac{1}{a^{2\alpha_{m,n}}}+|d_{n}|^{2}\frac{1}{a^{2(\alpha_{m,n}-1)}}\right)\leq\Gamma\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{n}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\alpha}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\alpha}\right)dx.

Therefore, the
n^{3}
growth turns into a growth in
|n|
, and this matches the growth found in (
3
). This estimate for
\dfrac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}u
follows also and this concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

4
Bochner’s Identity and the Eigenvalue Estimate

Corollary 4.1
.

Fix
3\leq m<\infty
. For all
0<\gamma<1
and for all
\gamma/2\leq\beta<\min\left\{\dfrac{m-1}{4},1\right\}
, there exists a constant
\Lambda_{\gamma}<\infty
such that for all
0<a<b<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\Lambda_{\gamma},

the following property is verified. Define
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
. There exists a constant
C_{m,\beta,\gamma}<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left|\nabla u+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{m,\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\right).

(4.1)

Proof.

Make the decomposition
u=u_{1}+u_{2}
, where
u_{1}
solves the elliptic system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathrm{Re}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{L}_{m}}^{\ast}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)\mathfrak{L}_{m}\right)u_{1}&=0\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega\\
u_{1}&=u\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega\end{aligned}\right.

(4.2)

that we obtain by direct minimisation of

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{m}(v)=\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}v|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

on
W^{2,2}_{u}(\Omega)
. In particular,
u_{2}\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)
, which implies by Theorem
2.2
that for all
0<\gamma<1
(since
\dfrac{m-1}{2}\geq 1
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left|\nabla u_{2}+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u_{2}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{m,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{2}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

(4.3)

Furthermore, since
u_{1}
minimises
\mathfrak{E}_{m}
on
W^{2,2}_{u}(\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{1}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{2}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{1}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle+2\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 4\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

(4.4)

Furthermore, the proof of Theorem
3.1
shows that the theorem also holds for solutions of (
4.2
) (the explanation is that adding the weight
a priori
in the PDE has the same effect to shift the frequencies as if we added them
a posteriori
)
so we estimate for all
0<\gamma<1
and
0<\beta<\mathrm{min}\left\{\dfrac{m-1}{4},1\right\}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left|\nabla u_{1}+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u_{1}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{m,\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Omega}(\mathscr{L}_{m}u_{1})^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{1}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\right).

(4.5)

We first have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{1}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+2\int_{\Omega}\frac{u_{2}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+C_{m,\beta}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{2}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+C_{m,\beta}\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{2}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx+4\,C_{m,\beta}\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

provided that
4\beta\geq 2\gamma
, where we used (
4
).
Then, notice that for all
v\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}v|^{2}|z|^{2\alpha}|dz|^{2}=\int_{\Omega}z^{\alpha}\overline{z}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}^{2}v\cdot\partial_{\overline{z}}^{2}v|dz|^{2}=-\int_{\Omega}z^{\alpha}\overline{z}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}(\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}v)\cdot\partial_{\overline{z}}v\,|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle-\alpha\int_{\Omega}z^{\alpha}\overline{z}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{z}^{2}v\cdot\partial_{\overline{z}}v\,|dz|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|z|^{2\alpha}|\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}v|^{2}|dz|^{2}+\alpha\int_{\Omega}z^{\alpha-1}\overline{z}^{\alpha}\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}v\cdot\partial_{\overline{z}}u\,|dz|^{2}-\alpha\int_{\Omega}z^{\alpha}\overline{z}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{z}^{2}v\cdot\partial_{\overline{z}}v|dz|^{2}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|z|^{2\alpha}|\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}v|^{2}|dz|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|z|^{2\alpha}|\partial_{z\overline{z}}^{2}v|^{2}|dz|^{2}+\frac{3\alpha^{2}}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\partial_{z}v|^{2}}{|z|^{2}}|z|^{2\alpha}|dz|^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\int_{\Omega}|z|^{2\alpha}|\partial_{z}^{2}v|^{2}|dz|^{2},

and this implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\Delta v)^{2}|x|^{2\alpha}dx\leq 12\alpha^{2}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}v|^{2}|z|^{2\alpha}|dz|^{2}+12\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{z}^{2}v|^{2}|dz|^{2},

or

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u_{2}\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 12\gamma^{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left|\nabla u_{2}+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u_{2}\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle+12\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u_{2}|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\mathscr{L}_{m}u_{1})^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle+2\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u_{2}\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 2\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle+C_{m,\gamma}\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx.

(4.6)

Therefore, the estimate follows from (
4.3
), (
4
), and (
4
).
∎

5
Morse Triviality of Variations Located in the Neck

In this section, generalising
[
18
, Theorem 1.4.1 p. 52]
, we will show that there cannot be negative variations whose support is located in the neck regions.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\{\vec{\Phi}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of Willmore immersions such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}W(\vec{\Phi}_{k})<\infty.

Let
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}}(0)
be a neck region, and let
m\geq 1
be the integer multiplicity of the branch point of the limiting bubble. Then, for all
1/2<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
there exists a universal constant
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0}(\beta_{1},\beta_{2})>0
such that for
k
large enough, we have for all
u_{k}\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{0}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u_{k}^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\lambda_{0}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

Proof.

Recall that by
[
19
]
, there exists a bounded function
\mu_{k}\in C^{0}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))
such that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{k}}=e^{\mu_{k}}|x|^{m-1}\qquad\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha).

(5.1)

In particular, there exists a constant
A\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle e^{-A}|x|^{m-1}\leq e^{\lambda_{k}}\leq e^{A}|x|^{m-1}.

Therefore, we deduce in particular that for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}e^{-2\lambda_{k}}(\Delta u)^{2}dx\geq e^{-A}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|x|^{2-2m}(\Delta u)^{2}dx.

Letting
v=|x|^{m-1}u\in W^{2,2}_{0}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|x|^{2-2m}(\Delta u_{k})^{2}dx=\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\left(\Delta v+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla v+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}v\right)^{2}dx.

Now, thanks to
[
18
, Corollary 2.1.2 p. 93, Corollary 2.1.5 p. 94]
, for
k
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\left(\Delta v+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla v+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}v\right)^{2}dx\geq\left(4m^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{v^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\left(\Delta v+2(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla v+\frac{(m-1)^{2}}{|x|^{2}}v\right)^{2}dx\geq\frac{4m^{2}+\dfrac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}}{4(m^{2}+1)+\dfrac{2\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}}\frac{\pi^{2}}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla v|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

On the other hand, Theorem
2.1
shows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\mathscr{L}_{m}v)^{2}dx\geq C_{m,\beta_{1},1}^{-1}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{v^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\mathscr{L}_{m}v)^{2}dx\geq C_{m,\beta_{2},2}^{-1}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla v|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

Noticing that

\displaystyle\nabla v=\nabla\left(|x|^{1-m}u\right)=\frac{\nabla u_{k}}{|x|^{m-1}}-(m-1)u\frac{x}{|x|^{m+1}},

all inequalities finally show that for some
C_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq C_{0}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle+C_{0}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(5.2)

Furthermore, inequality
[
18
, (1.2.14) p. 18]
shows that for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-2\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-2\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

(5.3)

Now, for every
0<\beta<1
, and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, thanks to
[
18
, Theorem 1.3.30 p. 51]
there exists
C_{l}(\beta)<\infty
such that for small enough
0<\alpha<1
and large enough
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle|x|^{l}|\nabla^{l}\vec{n}_{k}(x)|\leq C_{k}(\beta)\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\qquad\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha).

Therefore, fix
1/2<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
. We have

\displaystyle|A_{k}|\leq\frac{C_{0}^{\prime}(\beta_{1})}{|x|^{m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}

\displaystyle|\nabla A_{k}|\leq\frac{C_{1}^{\prime}(\beta_{2})}{|x|^{m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}.

Therefore, we get the following estimates for all
u\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u_{k}|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{4}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{4}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{4}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(5.4)

Then, we get by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\nabla u||u|}{|x|^{2m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\varepsilon\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.5)

Now, we need to choose
1/2<\beta<1
and
0<\varepsilon<1
such that
\beta_{1}=(1-\varepsilon)\beta>1/2
,
\beta_{2}=2\varepsilon\beta>1/2
, which shows that we need to choose
\beta>3/4
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\beta}<\varepsilon<1-\frac{1}{2\beta}.

Therefore, we deduce by (
5
), (
5
), and (
5
) that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}C_{0}-C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\left(1+\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}^{2}\right)\right)\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\right)dx

(5.6)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{4}C_{0}-C\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}\right)\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)dx.

(5.7)

Thanks to the quantization of energy (
[
5
]
), the result follows. Indeed, notice that as
\varepsilon\rightarrow\dfrac{1}{4\beta}
,
\beta_{2}\rightarrow 1/2
, and as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 1-\dfrac{1}{2\beta}
,
\beta_{1}\rightarrow 1/2
, so the inequality is verified for all
1/2<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
(and need not satisfy any relation between each other).
∎

Then, recall the formula for the second derivative of the Willmore energy:

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}}(u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g}u+|A|^{2}u\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(|du|_{h}^{2}+4|h_{0}|_{\mathrm{WP}}u^{2}\right)H^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g}-8\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}

\displaystyle-16\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial H,h_{0}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g}.

(5.8)

As in
[
18
, p. 56]
, fix
0<\beta_{1}<\min\left\{\dfrac{m-1}{4},1\right\}
and introduce the weight

\displaystyle\omega_{1,\alpha,k}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{4m}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{4m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}(0)}\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{4m}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+1+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

Introducing the operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g_{k}}^{2}+|A_{k}|^{2}\Delta_{g_{k}}+2\langle d|A_{k}|,d(\,\cdot\,)\rangle_{g_{k}}+2\,d^{\ast_{g_{k}}}\left(H_{k}^{2}\,d(\,\cdot\,)\right)+\left(|A_{k}|^{4}+\Delta_{g_{k}}|A_{k}|^{2}+24|H_{k}|^{2}|h_{0,k}|_{\mathrm{WP}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+16\,\ast_{g_{k}}\,d\,\mathrm{Re}\,\left(g_{k}^{-1}\otimes h_{0,k}\otimes\partial\left(H_{k}\,\cdot\,\right)\right)-16\langle\partial(\,\cdot\,)\otimes\partial H_{k},h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}.

Then,
\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}
is a self-adjoint operator and the following identity holds for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

In particular, if we define
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}=\omega_{1,\alpha,k}^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}
, we deduce that
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}
is a self-adjoint operator with respect to the weight
\omega_{1,\alpha,k}
and that

\displaystyle Q_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}u\,\mathcal{L}_{g_{k}}u\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

The next lemma readily follows from the general analysis given in
[
12
, Lemma IV.3]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
0<\alpha<1
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
let

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)=W^{2,2}(\Sigma)\cap\left\{u:\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,k}u=\lambda\,u\right\}.

Then, for all
0<\alpha<1
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the following formula holds true

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{W}(\vec{\Phi}_{k})=\mathrm{dim}\bigoplus_{\lambda<0}\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda).

We also need to make precise the Bochner identity in conformal coordinates.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Omega_{k}(\alpha)
be the neck region as above. Then, for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))
, if
g_{k}=\vec{\Phi}_{k}^{\ast}g_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}=e^{2\lambda_{k}}dx
is the induced metric, we have

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}=e^{-4\lambda_{k}}\left(|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+2(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\lambda_{k})\Delta u-2\,\nabla\lambda_{k}\cdot\nabla|\nabla u|^{2}\right).

(5.9)

Proof.

Since the metric is conformal, we have
g^{i,j}=e^{-2\lambda_{k}}\delta_{i,j}
, which yields

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}e^{-4\lambda_{k}}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}u}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\sum_{l=1}^{2}\Gamma_{i,j}^{l}\frac{\partial u}{\partial x_{l}}\right)^{2},

where
\Gamma_{i,j}^{l}
are the Christoffel symbols. Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i,j}^{l}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}g^{1,l}\left(\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial x_{i}}+\frac{\partial g_{1,i}}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\partial g_{i,j}}{\partial x_{1}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}g^{2,l}\left(\frac{\partial g_{j,2}}{\partial x_{i}}+\frac{\partial g_{2,i}}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\partial g_{i,j}}{\partial x_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{1,l}\left(\delta_{1,j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}+\delta_{1,i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}-\delta_{i,j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\right)\lambda_{k}+\delta_{2,l}\left(\delta_{2,j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}+\delta_{i,2}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}-\delta_{i,j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}\right)\lambda_{k}.

Therefore, we successively compute

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \Gamma_{1,1}^{1}&=\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{1}}\\
\Gamma_{1,1}^{2}&=-\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{2}}\\
\Gamma_{1,2}^{1}&=\Gamma_{2,1}^{1}=\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{2}}\\
\end{aligned}\right.\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} \Gamma_{1,2}^{2}&=\Gamma_{2,1}^{2}=\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{1}}\\
\Gamma_{2,2}^{1}&=-\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{1}}\\
\Gamma_{2,2}^{2}&=\frac{\partial\lambda_{k}}{\partial x_{2}}.\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, we deduce (removing the index
k
on the right-hand side for simplicity of notation) that

\displaystyle e^{4\lambda_{k}}|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u-\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}u+\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\left(\partial_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{2}u-\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}u+\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}-2\,\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{1}}u+2\,\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{2}}u

\displaystyle-4\,\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-4\,\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{2}}u

\displaystyle+2\,\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-2\,\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}u\,\partial_{x_{2}}u

\displaystyle=|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}-\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}|\partial_{x_{1}}u|^{2}+2\,\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\Delta u\,\partial_{x_{2}}u-\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}|\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}

\displaystyle-2\,\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}|\partial_{x_{1}}u|^{2}-2\,\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}|\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}

\displaystyle+2\,\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\Delta u\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-\partial_{x_{1}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{1}}|\partial_{x_{1}}u|^{2}-\partial_{x_{2}}\lambda\,\partial_{x_{2}}|\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}

\displaystyle=|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+2(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\lambda)\Delta u-2\nabla\lambda\cdot\nabla|\nabla u|^{2}.

∎

Next, we generalise
[
18
, Lemma 1.4.4]
to the case of branch points. This is where our Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality proves crucial.

Corollary 5.4
.

Fix
3\leq m<\infty
. For all
0<\gamma<1
and for all
\gamma/2\leq\beta<\min\left\{\dfrac{m-1}{4},1\right\}
, there exists a constant
\Lambda_{\gamma}<\infty
such that for all
0<a<b<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)\geq\Lambda_{\gamma},

the following property is verified. Define
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
. There exists a constant
C_{m,\beta,\gamma}<\infty
such that for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\left|\nabla u+(m-1)\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq C_{m,\beta,\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Omega}\left(\mathscr{L}_{m}u\right)^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx+\int_{\Omega}\left|\mathfrak{L}_{m}u\right|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\right).

(5.10)

Lemma 5.5
.

There exists
0<\alpha_{0}<\infty
,
0<\mu_{0}<\infty
, and a family of constants
\left\{\mu_{\alpha,k}\right\}_{0<\alpha<\alpha_{0},k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\mu_{0})
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{\alpha,k}=0,

with the following property: for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}\,\mathscr{E}_{\alpha,k}(\lambda)>0\implies\lambda\geq-\mu_{\alpha,k}\geq-\mu_{0}.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality (
[
11
]
) that the multiplicity of the limiting branch point is
m\geq 3
.
Thanks to the estimate of
[
18
, Theorem 1.3.30]
, and the expansion (
5.1
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|A_{k}(x)|\leq\frac{C(\beta_{1})}{|x|^{m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\alpha}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}\leq C^{\prime}(\beta_{1})\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}=0

thanks to the no-neck energy property. Then, by the strong convergence on
\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\widetilde{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}=\alpha^{m}\left\|A_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}.

Now, since
\vec{\Phi}_{\infty}
has a branch point of order
m
at
0
, Bernard–Rivière’s Taylor expansion (
[
4
]
) shows that there exists an integer
\theta_{0}\leq m-1
such that

\displaystyle\vec{H}_{\infty}=\mathrm{Re}\,\left(\frac{\vec{C}_{0}}{z^{\theta_{0}}}\right)+O(|z|^{1-\theta_{0}-\varepsilon})\qquad\text{for all}\;\,\varepsilon>0.

Likewise, we have
\vec{h}_{0,\infty}=O(|z|^{m-1})
, which implies that
A_{\infty}(x)=O(|x|^{1-m})
, so that (as
A_{\infty}
is smooth on
\Sigma\setminus\left\{0\right\}
)

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\alpha^{m}\left\|A_{\infty}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha))}=0.

Therefore, we need only prove the estimate on the bubble region. The bubble-neck decomposition implies that the sequence of immersions
\vec{\Psi}_{k}:B(0,\alpha^{-1})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}_{k}(x)=e^{-\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\left(\vec{\Phi}_{k}(\rho_{k}x)-\vec{\Phi}_{k}(0)\right)

is such that

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}\qquad\text{in}\;\,C^{l}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\widehat{\mathbb{C}})\;\,\text{for all}\;\,l\in\mathbb{N}.

Notice that we have

\displaystyle e^{-A}\left(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}\right)^{m-1}\leq e^{\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\leq e^{A}\left(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}\right)^{m-1}.

(5.11)

Now, let
\vec{\Phi}:B(0,1)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a smooth conformal immersion,
\rho,\mu>0
and
\vec{\Psi}:B(0,\rho^{-1})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be such that

\displaystyle\vec{\Psi}(x)=e^{-\mu}\vec{\Phi}(\rho\,x)\qquad\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\rho^{-1}).

The conformal factor of
\vec{\Psi}
is given by

\displaystyle e^{2\lambda_{\vec{\Psi}}(x)}=\frac{1}{2}|\nabla\vec{\Psi}(x)|^{2}=e^{-2\mu}\rho^{2}e^{2\lambda_{\vec{\Phi}}(\rho\,x)}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\vec{\Psi}(x)=e^{-\mu}\rho^{2}\Delta\vec{\Phi}(\rho\,x).

Therefore, its mean curvature is given by

\displaystyle\vec{H}_{\vec{\Psi}}(x)=\frac{1}{2}e^{-2\lambda_{\vec{\Psi}}(x)}\Delta\vec{\Psi}(x)=\frac{1}{2}e^{2\mu}\rho^{-2}e^{-2\lambda_{\vec{\Phi}}(\rho\,x)}\,e^{-\mu}\rho^{2}\Delta\vec{\Phi}(\rho\,x)=e^{\mu}\,\vec{H}_{\vec{\Phi}}(\rho\,x),

which shows in particular that

\displaystyle\int_{B(0,\rho^{-1})}|\vec{H}_{\vec{\Psi}}(x)|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Psi}}}(x)=\int_{B(0,\rho^{-1})}e^{2\mu}|\vec{H}_{\vec{\Phi}}(\rho\,x)|^{2}e^{-2\mu}\rho^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Phi}}}(\rho\,x)=\int_{B(0,1)}|\vec{H}_{\vec{\Phi}}|^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\vec{\Phi}}},

that also comes from the conformal invariance of the Willmore energy. Therefore, we deduce that in the example above, we have

\displaystyle\vec{H}_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)=e^{\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\vec{H}_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(\rho_{k}x).

Therefore, we get by the same argument

\displaystyle\frac{A_{k}(\rho_{k}x)}{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}(\delta_{k}x)^{\frac{1}{4}}}=(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{m}e^{-\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

By the strong bubble convergence,
A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}A_{\vec{\Psi}_{\infty}}(x)
for all
x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1})
, which implies in particular that

\displaystyle\left\|A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))}\leq C_{\alpha},

and we deduce by (
5.11
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{m}e^{A}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{1-m}\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle=C_{\alpha}\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{4\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}0.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}=0.

(5.12)

Notice that this result can be slightly refined. Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{m}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}(0)}\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{m}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta_{1}}+1+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\mu_{\alpha,k}=\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\widetilde{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}.

(5.13)

The same argument as above shows that there exists
\alpha_{0}>0
and
\mu_{0}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{\alpha,k}=0\\
&\text{For all}\;\,k\in\mathbb{N},\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0<\alpha<\alpha_{0},\;\,\text{we have}\;\,0<\mu_{\alpha,k}<\mu_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.14)

Furthermore, the elementary inequality
(a+b+c)^{2}\leq 3(a^{2}+b^{2}+c^{2})
applied twice
\widetilde{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}^{4}\leq 27\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}
.
Now, define a second weight
\omega_{2,\alpha,k}\in C^{0}(\Sigma)
by

\displaystyle\omega_{2,\alpha,k}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{4m-2}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{4m-2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{4m}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta_{2}}+1+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

(5.15)

Now, let us prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{|A_{k}||\nabla A_{k}|}{(\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k})^{\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)}=0.

(5.16)

First, on the neck region, we have for all
0<\beta<1
the estimates for all
x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)

\displaystyle|A_{k}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\beta}}{|x|^{m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}

\displaystyle|\nabla A_{k}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\beta}}{|x|^{m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\beta}+\frac{1}{\log\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(2\alpha))}.

Now, we also have for a universal constant
0<\Lambda_{\beta}<\infty

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\beta}^{-1}\omega_{3,\alpha,k}=(\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k})^{\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}}\leq\Lambda_{\beta}\,\omega_{3,\alpha,k},

where

\displaystyle\omega_{3,\alpha,k}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{2m+1}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{\gamma_{m}}+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{2m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{\gamma_{m}}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{\gamma_{m}}+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{\gamma_{m}}+1+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})\end{aligned}\right.

(5.17)

and

\displaystyle\gamma_{m}=\frac{2(2m+1)}{4m-1}\beta_{1}+\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}\beta_{2}=\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}\left(4\beta_{1}+2\beta_{2}\right).

In particular, we can replace
(\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k})^{\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}}
by
\omega_{3,\alpha,k}
in the limits to prove (
5.16
). Furthermore, notice that for all
m\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}<\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Furthermore, since

\displaystyle 6\gamma_{m}=\frac{3}{2}+\frac{9}{2(4m-1)},

for all

\displaystyle\frac{3}{8}+\frac{9}{8(4m-1)}<\beta<\frac{3}{4}+\frac{9}{4(4m-1)},

there exists
1/2<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}<1
such that
\gamma_{m}=2\beta
.
Therefore, we have for all
x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{k}(x)||\nabla A_{k}(x)|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}\leq C_{\beta}^{\prime}\left\|\nabla\vec{n}_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}^{2}\frac{\left(\dfrac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\dfrac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\dfrac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}}{\left(\dfrac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\dfrac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\dfrac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}}.

Notice that the fraction converges uniformly to
1
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, the no-neck energy property implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{|A_{k}||\nabla A_{k}|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

(5.18)

Then, we for all
x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)
a uniform convergence

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{k}(x)||\nabla A_{k}(x)|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}(x)}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\alpha^{2m+1}|A_{\infty}(x)||\nabla A_{\infty}(x)|.

By the previously mentioned second residue analysis of Bernard-Rivière, we have
A_{\infty}(x)=O(|x|^{1-m})
and
\nabla A_{\infty}(x)=O(|x|^{-m})
, which shows that
\alpha^{2m+1}|A_{\infty}||\nabla A_{\infty}|=O(\alpha^{2})
and finally that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{|A_{k}||\nabla A_{k}|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Omega_{k}(\alpha))}=0.

(5.19)

Finally, we investigate the behaviour on the bubble domain. We have

\displaystyle\nabla\vec{H}_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)=\rho_{k}e^{\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}\vec{H}_{\vec{\Phi}_{k}}(\rho_{k}x).

We deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{k}(\rho_{k}x)||\nabla A_{k}(\rho_{k}x)|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}=(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2m+1}\rho_{k}^{-1}e^{-2\lambda_{k}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})}|A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)||\nabla A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}(x)|\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-1}.

Thanks to the strong convergence of
A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}
and its derivatives, we deduce that for all
0<\alpha<1
there exists
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}<\infty
such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\||A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}||\nabla A_{\vec{\Psi}_{k}}|\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))}\leq C_{\alpha}^{\prime}.

Combining the estimate by (
5.11
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{k}(\rho_{k}x)||\nabla A_{k}(\rho_{k}x)|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2m+1}\rho_{k}^{-1}(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2(1-m)}\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=C_{\alpha}^{\prime}\alpha^{-3}\rho_{k}^{2}\left(1+\left(\alpha^{-2}\rho_{k}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{\frac{2m+1}{4m-1}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)^{-1}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}0

uniformly in
x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1})
. Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\frac{|A_{k}||\nabla A_{k}|}{\omega_{3,\alpha,k}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(B(0,\alpha^{-1}))}=0

(5.20)

and the limit (
5.16
) is established. We will actually need to refine this result. Now, defining

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}_{3,\alpha,k}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{2m+1}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{2m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\beta}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\beta}+1+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

Notice that we have
\widetilde{\omega}_{3,\alpha,k}\leq\omega_{3,\alpha,k}\leq\Gamma_{\beta}(\omega_{1,\alpha,k}\omega_{2,\alpha,k})^{\frac{2m+1}{2(4m-1)}}
.
Furthermore, if we define

\displaystyle\nu_{\alpha,k}=\left\|\frac{|A_{k}||\nabla A_{k}|}{\widetilde{\omega}_{3,\alpha,k}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)},

we deduce that there exists
0<\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha_{0}
and
\nu_{0}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\nu_{\alpha,k}=0\\
&\text{For all}\;\,k\in\mathbb{N},\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0<\alpha<\alpha_{1},\;\,\text{we have}\;\,0<\nu_{\alpha,k}<\nu_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.21)

Now, recall the formula (
5
). By Cauchy’s inequality, for all
u\in W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
we have the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u+|A_{k}|^{2}\right)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}|A_{k}|^{2}u\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

We estimate directly

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|A_{k}|^{4}u^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\leq\mu_{\alpha,k}^{4}\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\omega_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Likewise, if
0<\gamma<1
and

\displaystyle\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{\alpha^{2m}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\delta_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\gamma}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Sigma\setminus\overline{B}(0,\alpha)\\
&\frac{1}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)=B_{\alpha}\setminus\overline{B}_{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}(0)}\\
&\frac{1}{(\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k})^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{\alpha}\right)^{2\gamma}+1+\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B(0,\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}).\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\kappa_{\alpha,k}=\left\|\frac{A_{k}}{\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\Sigma)},

(5.22)

The same argument as above shows that there exists
0<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{1}
and
\kappa_{0}<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}\kappa_{\alpha,k}=0\\
&\text{For all}\;\,k\in\mathbb{N},\;\,\text{for all}\;\,0<\kappa<\kappa_{0},\;\,\text{we have}\;\,0<\kappa_{\alpha,k}<\mu_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.23)

we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle\partial u\otimes\partial u,h_{0,k}\rangle_{\mathrm{WP}}H_{k}\,d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|\leq\kappa_{\alpha,k}^{2}\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Now, integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=-\int_{\Sigma}u\,\Delta_{g_{k}}u\,\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}-\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u\,\nabla u\cdot\nabla\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}dx.

We directly estimate by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (assuming that
\beta\geq\beta_{1}
)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}u\,\Delta_{g_{k}}u\,\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{3}\left(\int_{\Sigma}u^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Sigma}(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

The second term is more delicate to estimate. We have for all
x\in\Omega_{k}(\alpha)

\displaystyle\nabla\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}=-2m\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\overline{\omega_{2,\alpha,k}}+2\gamma\frac{x}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\gamma}-\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)

which implies the elementary inequality

\displaystyle|\nabla\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}|\leq\frac{2(m+2\gamma)}{|x|}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}.

We first estimate by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u\nabla u\cdot\frac{x}{|x|^{2m+2}}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\rho_{k}}\right)}\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}u^{2}\omega_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality once more, we deduce that for all
0<\varepsilon<1

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|}{|x|^{m}}\frac{|u|}{|x|^{m+1}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{2\gamma}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{2\gamma}\right)dx\leq 2\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{\alpha}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\gamma}+\left(\frac{\alpha^{-1}\rho_{k}}{|x|}\right)^{4(1-\varepsilon)\gamma}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now, using Corollary (
5.4
), we deduce that for all
\varepsilon\gamma\leq\beta<\min\left\{\dfrac{m-1}{4},1\right\}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\beta,2\varepsilon\gamma}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2m+2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\beta}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\beta}\right)dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m-2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)dx\right)

Now, recall Bochner’s identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{g_{k}}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle=|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}+\langle du,d\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\rangle_{g_{k}}+\mathrm{Ric}_{g_{k}}(du,du)

\displaystyle=|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}+\langle du,d\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\rangle_{g_{k}}+2K_{g_{k}}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}.

Integrating by parts, we deduce as
\Sigma
is closed that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\Delta_{g_{k}}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\int_{\Sigma}\left(|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|^{2}_{g_{k}}+\langle du,d\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)\rangle_{g_{k}}+2K_{g}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|^{2}_{g_{k}}-(\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}+2K_{g_{k}}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left|\mathrm{Hess}(u)\right|^{2}_{g_{k}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left(\Delta_{g_{k}}u\right)^{2}-2K_{g_{k}}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Therefore, we deduce that we deduce that for some universal
0<C<\infty

\displaystyle C^{-1}\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\frac{|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}}{|x|^{2m-2}}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)dx\leq\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}e^{-4\lambda_{k}}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}\left(|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}-2\langle du,d\lambda_{k}\rangle_{g_{k}}\Delta_{g_{k}}u+2\,e^{-2\lambda_{k}}\langle d\lambda_{k},d|\nabla u|^{2}\rangle_{g_{k}}\right)\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

We first have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}\leq\int_{\Sigma}|\mathrm{Hess}(u)|_{g_{k}}^{2}=\int_{\Sigma}\left((\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}-2K_{g_{k}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}

Now, we have
2|K_{g_{k}}|\leq|A_{k}|\leq\kappa_{\alpha,k}\overline{\omega_{1,\alpha,k}}
, so this term can be absorbed back in the previous estimate of

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\overline{\omega}_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Sigma}\langle du,d\lambda_{k}\rangle_{g_{k}}\Delta_{g_{k}}u\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{4\varepsilon\gamma}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\Sigma}|du|_{g_{k}}^{2}\left(\left(\frac{|x|}{b}\right)^{8\varepsilon\gamma}+\left(\frac{a}{|x|}\right)^{8\varepsilon\gamma}\right)d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{k}(\alpha)}-\Delta_{g_{k}}u)^{2}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}e^{-2\lambda_{k}}\langle d\lambda_{k},d|\nabla u|^{2}\rangle_{g_{k}}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=-\int_{\Sigma}\Delta_{\alpha_{k}}\lambda_{k}|\nabla u|^{2}_{\alpha_{k}}d\mathrm{vol}_{\alpha_{k}},

where
\alpha_{k}
is a smooth metric of constant curvature, and using the Liouville equation, this term can be estimated as above and this concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We can now move to the proof of the main theorem (Theorem
1.3
) of the article.

Proof.

We follow the lines of the proof of the main theorem of
[
18
]
. They are unchanged up to one important point that renormalising the variations to

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}u_{k}^{2}\,\omega_{1,\alpha,k}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{k}}=1,

this condition need not hold in the limit, but thanks to Fatou’s lemma,
u_{k}\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\infty}
in
W^{2,2}(\Sigma)
and that

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}u_{\infty}^{2}\omega_{1,\alpha,\infty}d\mathrm{vol}_{g_{\infty}}\leq 1.

In particular,
u
is an admissible variation (see
[
15
]
) since it vanishes sufficiently quickly at branch points, which shows that it is a genuine negative variation of the limiting immersion of the bubble. It cannot vanish identically either since constant functions yield a positive variation for a Willmore immersion. The argument by contradiction can therefore be reproduced
mutadis mutandis
which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎
